**Rwanda**,[^cite_note-17] officially the **Republic of Rwanda**,[^cite_note-18] is a landlocked country in [East Africa](260987.md). Its geography is dominated by mountains in the west and savanna in the southeast. The largest and most notable lakes are mainly in the western and northern regions of the country, and several volcanoes that form part of the [Virunga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virunga_Mountains) volcanic chain are primarily in the northwest. The climate is considered [tropical highland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highland_temperate_climate), with two rainy seasons and two dry seasons each year. Its [capital](181337.md) and largest city is [Kigali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kigali), located at the centre of the country, at 1,567 [metres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metre) above sea level. Rwanda lies a few degrees south of the [Equator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equator) in the [Great Rift Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Rift_Valley) of [East Africa](260987.md). Rwanda is bordered by [Uganda](31816.md) to the north, [Tanzania](30118.md) to the east, [Burundi](21490998.md) to the south, and the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](76762.md) to the west. With a population of about 14 million people living within a total area of 26,338 square kilometres (10,169 sq mi), of which land accounts for about 93.7%, Rwanda is the [21st most densely](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density) populated country in the world, with an average of about 578 people per square kilometre (1,500 per square mile).

[Hunter-gatherers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer) settled the territory in the [Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Age) and [Iron Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age), followed later by [Bantu peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bantu_peoples). The population coalesced first into [clans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clans_of_Rwanda), and then into kingdoms. In the 15th century, one kingdom, under [King Gihanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gihanga), managed to incorporate several of its close neighbour territories establishing the [Kingdom of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Rwanda). The Kingdom of Rwanda dominated from the mid-eighteenth century, with its Tutsi kings conquering others militarily, centralising power, and enacting unifying policies. In 1897, [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_colonial_empire) colonized Rwanda as part of [German East Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_East_Africa), followed by [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_colonial_empire), which took control in 1916 during [World War I](4764461.md). Both European nations ruled through the Rwandan king and perpetuated a pro-Tutsi policy. The Hutu population [revolted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Revolution) in 1959. They massacred numerous Tutsi and ultimately established an independent, Hutu-dominated republic in 1962 led by President [Grégoire Kayibanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gr%C3%A9goire_Kayibanda). A [1973 military coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Rwandan_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) overthrew Kayibanda and brought [Juvénal Habyarimana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana) to power, who retained the pro-Hutu policy. The Tutsi-led [Rwandan Patriotic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Patriotic_Front) (RPF) launched a [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Civil_War) in 1990. Habyarimana was [assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_and_Cyprien_Ntaryamira) in April 1994 by unknown parties. Social tensions erupted in the [Rwandan genocide](676659.md) carried out by [Hutu Power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutu_Power) extremists against the Tutsis. The RPF ended the genocide with a military victory in July 1994.

Rwanda has been governed by the RPF as a *[de facto](43594.md)* [one-party state](102459.md) since 1994 with former commander [Paul Kagame](393593.md) serving as president since 2000. The country has been governed by a series of centralized [authoritarian governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism) since precolonial times. Although Rwanda has low levels of [corruption](3608404.md) compared with neighbouring countries, it ranks among the lowest in international measurements of government transparency and civil liberties, despite recent gains that have elevated it to the medium category on the [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index). The population is [young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_in_Rwanda) and predominantly rural; Rwanda has one of the youngest populations in the world. Rwandans are drawn from just one cultural and linguistic group, the [Banyarwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banyarwanda). However, within this group there are three subgroups: the [Hutu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutu), [Tutsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutsi), and [Twa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes_Twa). The Twa are forest-dwelling [Central African foragers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Pygmies) and are often considered descendants of Rwanda's earliest inhabitants. Christianity is the largest religion in the country; the principal and national language is [Kinyarwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinyarwanda), spoken by native Rwandans, with [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language), [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language), and [Swahili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swahili) serving as additional official languages.

Rwanda's economy is based on services, agricultural exports, and manufacturing. Coffee and tea are the major [cash crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cash_crop) that it exports, although it is surpassed in banana production. [Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Rwanda) is a fast-growing sector and is now the country's leading foreign exchange earner. As of the most recent survey in 2024, 30.5% of the population is affected by [multidimensional poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multidimensional_Poverty_Index) with 27.4% under the national poverty line. The country is a member of the [African Union](10634933.md), the [United Nations](31769.md), the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations) (one of the few members without a historical link to the [British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire)), [COMESA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Market_for_Eastern_and_Southern_Africa), the [OIF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie), and the [East African Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Community).

## Etymology

The name *Rwanda* derives from the word Ku-aanda, meaning "expansion" or "spreading out from the centre", reflecting the historical growth of the [Kingdom of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Rwanda).[^cite_note-19]

## History

Main article: [History of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Rwanda)

### Early history



A reconstruction of the ancient King's Palace at [Nyanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nyanza,_Rwanda)

Modern human settlement of what is now Rwanda dates from, at the latest, the [last glacial period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_glacial_period), either in the [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) period around 8,000 BC, or in the [long humid period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic_Subpluvial) which followed, up to around 3,000 BC.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200344-20] Archaeological excavations have revealed evidence of sparse settlement by [hunter-gatherers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer) in the late [Stone Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Age), followed by a larger population of early [Iron Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age) settlers, who produced dimpled [pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pottery) and iron tools.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDorsey199436-21][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200345-22] These early inhabitants were the ancestors of the [Twa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes_Twa), aboriginal [pygmy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pygmy_peoples) hunter-gatherers who remain in Rwanda today. Then by 3,000 BC, [Central Sudanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Sudanic) and [Kuliak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuliak_languages) farmers and herders began settling into Rwanda, followed by [South Cushitic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Cushitic_languages)–speaking herders in 2,000 BC.[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261-25] The forest-dwelling Twa lost much of their habitat and moved to the mountain slopes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200775-26] Between 800 BC and 1500 AD, a number of [Bantu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bantu_peoples) groups migrated into Rwanda, clearing forest land for agriculture.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261-25][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200358-27] Historians have several theories regarding the nature of the Bantu migrations; one theory is that the first settlers were [Hutu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutu), while the [Tutsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutsi) migrated later to form a distinct racial group, possibly of Nilo-hamitic origin.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199516-28] An alternative theory is that the migration was slow and steady, with incoming groups integrating into rather than conquering the existing society.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200261-25][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200258-29] Under this theory, the Hutu and Tutsi distinction arose later and was a class distinction rather than a racial one.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200369-30][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShyaka10–11-31] The earliest form of social organisation in the area was the [clan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clans_of_Rwanda) (*ubwoko*).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200388-32] The clans were not limited to genealogical lineages or geographical area, and most included Hutu, Tutsi, and Twa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien200388–89-33] From the 15th century, the clans began to merge into kingdoms.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003141-34]

### Kingdom of Rwanda

Main article: [Kingdom of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Rwanda)

One kingdom, under King Gihanga, managed to incorporate several of its close neighbour territories establishing the [Kingdom of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Rwanda). By 1700, around eight kingdoms had existed in the present-day Rwanda.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003482-35] One of these, the [Kingdom of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Rwanda) ruled by the Tutsi [Nyiginya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nyiginya) clan, became increasingly dominant from the mid-18th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003160-36] The kingdom reached its greatest extent during the 19th century under the reign of King [Kigeli Rwabugiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kigeli_IV_Rwabugiri). Rwabugiri conquered several smaller states, expanded the kingdom west and north,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003160-36][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269-37] and initiated administrative reforms; these included *[ubuhake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ubuhake)*, in which Tutsi patrons ceded cattle, and therefore privileged status, to Hutu or Tutsi clients in exchange for economic and personal service,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199513–14-38] and *uburetwa*, a [corvée](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corv%C3%A9e) system in which Hutu were forced to work for Tutsi chiefs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269-37] Rwabugiri's changes caused a rift to grow between the Hutu and Tutsi populations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200269-37] The Twa were better off than in pre-Kingdom days, with some becoming dancers in the royal court,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200775-26] but their numbers continued to decline.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19956-39]

### German colonisation

Main article: [German East Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_East_Africa)

The [Berlin Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlin_Conference) of 1884 assigned the territory to the [German Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Empire), who declared it to be part of [German East Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_East_Africa). In 1894, explorer [Gustav Adolf von Götzen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustav_Adolf_von_G%C3%B6tzen) was the first European to cross the entire territory of Rwanda; he crossed from the south-east to Lake Kivu and met the king.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003217-40][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19959-41] In 1897, Germany established a presence in Rwanda with the formation of an alliance with the king, beginning the colonial era.[^cite_note-42] The Germans did not significantly alter the social structure of the country, but exerted influence by supporting the king and the existing hierarchy, and delegating power to local chiefs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199525-43][^cite_note-44]

### Belgian mandate of Ruanda-Urundi

Main article: [Ruanda-Urundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruanda-Urundi)

[Belgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_colonial_empire) forces invaded Rwanda and [Burundi](21490998.md) in 1916, during [World War I](4764461.md), and later, in 1922, they started to rule both Rwanda and Burundi as a [League of Nations mandate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Nations_mandate) called [Ruanda-Urundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruanda-Urundi) and started a period of more direct colonial rule.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003260-45] The Belgians simplified and centralised the power structure,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003270-46] introduced large-scale projects in education, health, public works, and agricultural supervision, including new crops and improved agricultural techniques to try to reduce the incidence of famine.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChrétien2003276–277-47] Both the Germans and the Belgians, in the wake of [New Imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Imperialism), promoted Tutsi supremacy, considering the Hutu and Tutsi different [races](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_\(human_categorization\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAppiahGates2010450-48] In 1935, Belgium introduced an identity card system, which labelled each individual as either Tutsi, Hutu, Twa or Naturalised. While it had been previously possible for particularly wealthy Hutu to become honorary Tutsi, the identity cards prevented any further movement between the classes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGourevitch200056–57-49]

Belgium continued to rule [Ruanda-Urundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruanda-Urundi) (of which Rwanda formed the northern part) as a [UN trust territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_trust_territories) after the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War), with a mandate to oversee eventual [independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decolonisation_of_Africa).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(II)-50][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(III)-51] Tensions escalated between the Tutsi, who favoured early independence, and the Hutu emancipation movement, culminating in the 1959 [Rwandan Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Revolution): Hutu activists began killing Tutsi and destroying their houses,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELindenLinden1977267-52] forcing more than 100,000 people to seek refuge in neighbouring countries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGourevitch200058–59-53][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199551-54]

### First Republic

Main article: [First Rwandese Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Rwandese_Republic)

In 1961, the suddenly pro-Hutu Belgians held [a referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1961_Rwandan_monarchy_referendum) in which the country voted to abolish the monarchy. Rwanda was separated from Burundi and gained independence on 1 July 1962,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199553-55] which is commemorated as Independence Day, a national holiday.[^cite_note-56] Cycles of violence followed, with mainly Tutsi [exile rebels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inyenzi_movement) attacking from neighbouring countries and the Hutu-led government retaliating with large-scale slaughter and repression of the Tutsi civilians inside Rwanda.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199556-57]

### Second Republic

Main article: [Second Rwandese Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Rwandese_Republic)

Photograph of President Juvénal Habyarimana arriving with entourage at Andrews Air Force Base, Maryland, USA on 25 September 1980.

[Juvénal Habyarimana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana), president from 1973 to 1994

In 1973, [Juvénal Habyarimana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana) took power in [a military coup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Rwandan_coup_d'%C3%A9tat). Pro-Hutu discrimination continued, but there was greater economic prosperity and a reduced amount of violence against the Tutsi.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199574–76-58] The Twa remained marginalised, and by 1990 were almost entirely forced out of the forests by the government; many became beggars.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNPO2008History-59] Rwanda's population had increased from 1.6 million people in 1934 to 7.1 million in 1989, leading to competition for land.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19954-60]

In 1990, the [Rwandan Patriotic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Patriotic_Front) (RPF), a rebel group composed of Tutsi refugees, invaded northern Rwanda from their base in Uganda, initiating the [Rwandan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Civil_War).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199593-61] The group condemned the Hutu-dominated government for failing to democratize and confront the problems facing these refugees. Neither side was able to gain a decisive advantage in the war,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995135–136-62] but by 1992 it had weakened Habyarimana's authority; mass demonstrations forced him into a coalition with the domestic opposition and eventually to sign the 1993 [Arusha Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arusha_Accords_\(Rwanda\)) with the RPF.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995190–191-63]

### Rwandan Genocide and Civil War

Main articles: [Rwandan genocide](676659.md), [Rwandan Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Civil_War), and [Third Rwandese Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Rwandese_Republic)



Human skulls at the [Nyamata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nyamata) Genocide Memorial.

The cease-fire ended on 6 April 1994 when [Habyarimana's plane was shot down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Juv%C3%A9nal_Habyarimana_and_Cyprien_Ntaryamira) near Kigali Airport, killing him.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2010a-64] The shooting down of the plane served as the catalyst for the [Rwandan genocide](676659.md), which began within a few hours. Over the course of approximately 100 days, between 500,000 and 1,000,000[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHenley2007-65] Tutsi and politically moderate Hutu were killed in well-planned attacks on the orders of the interim government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005386-66] Many Twa were also killed, despite not being directly targeted.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNPO2008History-59]

The Tutsi RPF restarted their offensive, and took control of the country methodically, gaining control of the whole country by mid-July.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005299-67] The international response to the genocide was limited, with major powers reluctant to strengthen the already overstretched [UN peacekeeping force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Assistance_Mission_for_Rwanda).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDallaire2005364-68] When the RPF took over, approximately two million Hutu [fled to neighbouring countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes_refugee_crisis), in particular [Zaïre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Za%C3%AFre), fearing reprisals;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier1995312-69] additionally, the RPF-led army was a key belligerent in the [First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Congo_War) and [Second Congo Wars](877492.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2010b-70] Within Rwanda, a period of reconciliation and justice began, with the establishment of the [International Criminal Tribunal for Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Tribunal_for_Rwanda) (ICTR) and the reintroduction of *[Gacaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gacaca)*, a traditional village court system.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBowcott2014-71]

### 21st century

In a bid to promote national unity and reconciliation, Rwanda's new [flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Rwanda) and [national anthem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_Nziza) were unveiled at the end of 2001, removing overt ethnic distinctions in official state identity. Since 2000 Rwanda's economy,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(X)-72] tourist numbers,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(XI)-73] and [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) have grown rapidly;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(I)2010-74] between 2006 and 2011 the poverty rate reduced from 57% to 45%,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2012-75] while [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) rose from 46.6 years in 2000[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(V)20132-76] to 65.4 years in 2021.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77]

## Politics and government

Main articles: [Politics of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Rwanda), [Foreign relations of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Rwanda), and [Elections in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Rwanda)

Photograph of Paul Kagame, taken in Busan, South Korea, in 2014

Rwandan President [Paul Kagame](393593.md)

Rwanda is a *[de facto](43594.md)* [one-party state](102459.md)[^cite_note-Yale_University_Press-78][^cite_note-The_Conversation-79][^cite_note-Handbook_of_Restorative_Justice-80][^cite_note-TWQ-81][^cite_note-University_of_Chicago_Press-82][^cite_note-jstor.org-83] ruled by the [Rwandan Patriotic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Patriotic_Front) (RPF) and its leader [Paul Kagame](393593.md) continuously since the end of the civil war in 1994.[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-85] Although Rwanda is nominally democratic, elections are manipulated in various ways, which include banning opposition parties, arresting or assassinating critics, and [electoral fraud](382165.md).[^cite_note-86] The RPF is a [Tutsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutsi)-dominated party but receives support from other communities as well.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClark2010-87]

The constitution was adopted following a national referendum in 2003, replacing the transitional constitution which had been in place since 1994.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPanapress2003-88] The constitution mandates a multi-party system of government, with politics based on democracy and elections.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_52-89] However, the constitution places conditions on how political parties may operate. Article 54 states that "political organizations are prohibited from basing themselves on race, ethnic group, tribe, clan, region, sex, religion or any other division which may give rise to discrimination".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_54-90] The [president of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Rwanda) is the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_98-91] and has broad powers including creating policy in conjunction with the [Cabinet of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Rwanda),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_117-92] commanding the [Rwandan Defence Force](1511714.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_110-93] negotiating and ratifying treaties,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_189-94] signing presidential orders,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_112-95] and declaring war or a state of emergency.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_110-93] The president is elected [every five years](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Rwanda),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003articles_100–101-96] and appoints the [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Rwanda) and all other members of the Cabinet.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_116-97] The [Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Rwanda) consists of two [chambers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_chamber). It makes legislation and is empowered by the constitution to oversee the activities of the president and the Cabinet.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_62-98] The lower chamber is the [Chamber of Deputies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_\(Rwanda\)), which has 80 members serving five-year terms. Twenty-four of these seats are reserved for women, elected through a joint assembly of local government officials; another three seats are reserved for youth and disabled members; the remaining 53 are elected by [universal suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_suffrage) under a [proportional representation](23298.md) system.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_76-99]

Photograph of the Chamber of Deputies with highway in the foreground

[Chamber of Deputies building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_Deputies_of_Rwanda)

Rwanda's legal system is largely based on [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Germany) and [Belgian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Belgium) [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) systems and [customary law](218798.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77] The judiciary is independent of the executive branch,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_140-100] although the president and the Senate are involved in the appointment of Supreme Court judges.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_148-101] Human Rights Watch has praised the Rwandan government for progress made in the delivery of justice including the abolition of the death penalty,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_WatchWells2008I._Summary-102] but also alleges interference in the judicial system by members of the government, such as the politically motivated appointment of judges, misuse of prosecutorial power, and pressure on judges to make particular decisions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHuman_Rights_WatchWells2008VIII._Independence_of_the_Judiciary-103] The constitution provides for two types of courts: ordinary and specialised.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_143-104] Ordinary courts are the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Rwanda), the [High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Court_of_Rwanda?action=edit\&redlink=1), and regional courts, while specialised courts are military courts[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_143-104] and a system of commercial courts created in 2011 to expedite commercial litigations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKamere2011-105] Between 2004 and 2012, a system of [*Gacaca* courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gacaca_court) was in operation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2015b-106] *Gacaca*, a Rwandan traditional court operated by villages and communities, was revived to expedite the trials of genocide suspects.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalkerMarch_2004-107] The court succeeded in clearing the backlog of genocide cases, but was criticised by human rights groups as not meeting legal fair standard.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2012a-108]

Rwanda has low corruption levels relative to most other African countries; in 2014, [Transparency International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transparency_International) ranked Rwanda as the fifth-cleanest out of 47 countries in [Sub-Saharan Africa](27067.md) and 55th-cleanest out of 175 in the world.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETransparency_International2014-109][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgutamba2014-110] The constitution provides for an [ombudsman](26289198.md), whose duties include prevention and fighting of corruption.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_182-111][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''Office_of_the_Ombudsman''-112] Public officials (including the president) are required by the constitution to declare their wealth to the ombudsman and to the public; those who do not comply are suspended from office.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsiimwe2011-113] Despite this, Human Rights Watch notes extensive political repression throughout the country, including illegal and arbitrary detention, threats or other forms of intimidation, disappearances, politically motivated trials, and the massacre of peacefully protesting civilians.[^cite_note-Roth-114]

Rwanda is a member of the [United Nations](31769.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_(I)-115] [African Union](10634933.md), [Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Francophonie),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrancophonie-116] [East African Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Community),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrainger2007-117] and the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFletcher2009-118] For many years during the Habyarimana regime, the country maintained close ties with France, as well as Belgium, the former colonial power.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199589-119] Under the RPF government, however, Rwanda has sought closer ties with neighbouring countries in the East African Community and with the English-speaking world. Diplomatic relations with France were suspended in 2006 following the indictment of Rwandan officials by a French judge,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPorter2008-120] and despite their restoration in 2010, as of 2015 relations between the countries remain strained.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEXinhua_News_Agency2015-121] Relations with the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](76762.md) were tense following Rwanda's involvement in the [First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Congo_War) and [Second Congo Wars](877492.md);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2010b-70] the Congolese army alleged Rwandan attacks on their troops, while Rwanda blamed the Congolese government for failing to suppress Hutu rebels in [North](446958.md) and [South Kivu](446959.md) provinces.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSA_Today2008-122][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl_Jazeera2007-123] In 2010, the United Nations released a report accusing the Rwandan army of committing wide scale human rights violations and crimes against humanity in the Democratic Republic of the Congo during the [First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Congo_War) and [Second Congo Wars](877492.md), charges denied by the Rwandan government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGreal2010-124] Relations soured further in 2012, as Kinshasa accused Rwanda of supporting the [M23 rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M23_rebellion_\(2012%E2%80%932013\)), an insurgency in the eastern Congo.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2012b-125] In 2015 peace had been restored and relations were improving,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgence_Africaine_de_Presse2015-126] but by January 2025 renewed Rwandan support for the M23 rebellion caused DR Congo to sever diplomatic relations in the wake of the [2025 Goma offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Goma_offensive).[^cite_note-127]

Rwanda's relationship with [Uganda](31816.md) was also tense for much of the 2000s following a 1999 clash between the two countries' armies as they backed opposing rebel groups in the Second Congo War,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeuler2011-128] but improved significantly in the early 2010s.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2011-129][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaboja2015-130] In 2019, relations between the two countries deteriorated, with Rwanda closing its borders with Uganda.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132]

On 25 January 2025, the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](76762.md) broke off diplomatic ties with Rwanda over its alleged support for the [M23 rebel group](35878650.md) operating in the country.[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134]

### Military

Main articles: [Rwandan Defence Force](1511714.md) and [Rwandan Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Air_Force)

The national military is the [Rwandan Defence Force](1511714.md) (RDF), which consists of the Rwanda Land Forces, the [Rwandan Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Air_Force), and specialized service branches.[^cite_note-135] The RDF was established following the 1994 civil war from the ranks of the [Rwandan Patriotic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Patriotic_Front) (RPF) rebel army, replacing the former government's *Forces Armées Rwandaises* (FAR). Enlistment in the RDF is entirely voluntary, and the minimum age for recruitment is 18 years. Military expenditure constitutes a notable portion of the national budget, reflecting the country's proactive regional security doctrine.

Despite its relatively small geographic size, Rwanda maintains one of the most operationally active militaries in East Africa. The country has evolved into one of the largest contributors of personnel to [United Nations peacekeeping](15652027.md) operations globally, consistently ranking among the top three troop-contributing countries alongside Nepal and Bangladesh.[^cite_note-136] RDF battalions and formed police units are heavily deployed in stabilization missions across the continent, most notably under the United Nations Multidimensional Integrated Stabilization Mission in the Central African Republic (MINUSCA) and previously under the African Union-United Nations Hybrid Operation in Darfur (UNAMID).[^cite_note-137]

In addition to multilateral UN mandates, Rwanda frequently utilizes independent bilateral military interventions to address regional crises. Since July 2021, the RDF has maintained a substantial deployment in northern [Mozambique](19301.md)'s Cabo Delgado province to combat Islamist insurgencies.[^cite_note-138] Following the withdrawal of the Southern African Development Community Mission in Mozambique (SAMIM) in 2024, the footprint expanded dramatically.[^cite_note-139] As of 2026, official statements from the deployment command indicated that more than 6,300 Rwandan soldiers and security personnel were active in the province to degrade insurgent capabilities and secure infrastructure.[^cite_note-140] Domestically, the RDF is highly institutionalized in civilian development projects, deploying military engineers, medical staff, and logistics units to build public infrastructure and schools during an annual outreach campaign known as the "RDF Citizen Production Army".

## Administrative divisions

Further information: [Decentralization in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decentralization_in_Rwanda)

Main article: [Provinces of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Rwanda)

Map of Rwanda showing the five provinces in various colours, as well as major cities, lakes, rivers, and areas of neighbouring countries

Provinces of Rwanda

Before western colonization, the Rwandan government system had a [quasi-system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quasi-state) of [political pluralism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pluralism_\(political_theory\)) and power sharing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOAU200014-141] Despite there being a strict hierarchy, the pre-colonial system achieved an established, combined system of "centralized power and decentralized autonomous units." Under the monarch, the elected Chief governed a province that was divided into multiple districts. Two other officials appointed by head Chief governed the districts; one official was allocated power over the land while the other oversaw cattle. The [king](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_kings_of_Rwanda) (*mwami*) exercised control through a system of provinces, districts, hills, and neighbourhoods.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMelvern20045-142] As of 2003, the constitution [divided](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Rwanda) Rwanda into [provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_Rwanda) (*intara*), [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Rwanda) (*uturere*), cities, municipalities, towns, [sectors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectors_of_Rwanda) (*imirenge*), cells (*utugari*), and villages (*imidugudu*); the larger divisions, and their borders, are established by Parliament.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_3-143] In January 2006, Rwanda was reorganized such that twelve provinces were merged to create five, and 106 districts were merged into thirty.[^cite_note-144] The present borders drawn in 2006 aimed at decentralising power and removing associations with the old system and the genocide. The previous structure of twelve provinces associated with the largest cities was replaced with five provinces based primarily on geography.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2006a-145] These are [Northern Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Province,_Rwanda), [Southern Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Province,_Rwanda), [Eastern Province](3624633.md), [Western Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Province,_Rwanda), and the Municipality of Kigali in the centre.

The five provinces act as intermediaries between the national government and their constituent districts to ensure that national policies are implemented at the district level. The Rwanda Decentralisation Strategic Framework developed by the Ministry of Local Government assigns to provinces the responsibility for "coordinating governance issues in the Province, as well as monitoring and evaluation".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20078-146] Each province is headed by a governor, appointed by the president and approved by the Senate.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthern_Province-147] The districts are responsible for coordinating public service delivery and economic development. They are divided into sectors, which are responsible for the delivery of public services as mandated by the districts.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20079-148] Districts and sectors have directly elected councils, and are run by an executive committee selected by that council.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC2004-149] The cells and villages are the smallest political units, providing a link between the people and the sectors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20079-148] All adult resident citizens are members of their local cell council, from which an executive committee is elected.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC2004-149] The city of [Kigali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kigali) is a provincial-level authority, which coordinates urban planning within the city.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINALOC20078-146]

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Rwanda)



Topography of Rwanda

Rwanda is located in [East Africa](260987.md), in the eastern-central part of the [African](5334607.md) continent, between 1° and 3° S latitude and between 29° and 31° E longitude. Its geography is characterized by rolling highlands, mountains, and numerous lakes. At 26,338 square kilometres (10,169 sq mi), Rwanda is the world's [144th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_area)-largest country, and the fourth smallest on the African mainland after [Gambia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gambia), [Eswatini](27451.md), and [Djibouti](17207794.md). It is comparable in size to [Burundi](21490998.md), [Haiti](13373.md) and [Albania](738.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERichards1994-150] The entire country is at a high altitude: the [lowest point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_lowest_point) is the [Rusizi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rusizi_River) at 950 metres (3,117 ft) above sea level.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77]

The [watershed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congo-Nile_Divide) between the major [Congo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congo_Basin) and [Nile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nile_Basin) [drainage basins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drainage_basin) runs from north to south through Rwanda, with around 80% of the country's area draining into the Nile and 20% into the Congo via the [Rusizi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rusizi_River) and [Lake Tanganyika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Tanganyika).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENile_Basin_Initiative2010-151] The country's longest river is the [Nyabarongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nyabarongo_River_Wetlands), which rises in the south-west, flows north, east, and southeast before merging with the [Ruvubu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruvubu_River) to form the [Kagera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kagera_River); the Kagera then flows due north along the eastern border with Tanzania. The Nyabarongo-Kagera eventually drains into [Lake Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Victoria), and its source in Nyungwe Forest is a contender for the as-yet undetermined overall [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_\(river_or_stream\)) of the [Nile](21244.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2006b-152] Rwanda has many lakes, the largest being [Lake Kivu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Kivu). This lake occupies the floor of the [Albertine Rift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albertine_Rift) along most of the length of Rwanda's western border, and with a maximum depth of 480 metres (1,575 ft),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJørgensen200593-153] it is one of the twenty [deepest lakes in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_by_depth).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006153-154] Other sizeable lakes include [Burera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Burera), [Ruhondo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ruhondo), [Muhazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Muhazi), [Rweru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Rweru), and [Ihema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ihema), the last being the largest of a string of lakes in the eastern plains of [Akagera National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akagera_National_Park).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHodd1994522-155]

Mountains dominate central and western Rwanda and the country is sometimes called "*Pays des mille collines*" in French ("Land of a thousand hills").[^cite_note-156] They are part of the Albertine Rift Mountains that flank the Albertine branch of the [East African Rift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Rift), which runs from north to south along Rwanda's western border.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWWF2001Location_and_General_Description-157] The highest peaks are found in the [Virunga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virunga_Mountains) volcano chain in the northwest; this includes [Mount Karisimbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Karisimbi), Rwanda's highest point, at 4,507 metres (14,787 ft).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMehtaKatee200537-158] This western section of the country lies within the [Albertine Rift montane forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albertine_Rift_montane_forests) ecoregion.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWWF2001Location_and_General_Description-157] It has an elevation of 1,500 to 2,500 metres (4,921 to 8,202 ft).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMunyakaziNtagaramba20057-159] The centre of the country is predominantly rolling hills, while the eastern border region consists of [savanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savanna), plains and swamps.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMunyakaziNtagaramba200518-160]

### Climate

Photograph of a lake with one of the Virunga mountains behind, partially in cloud

Lake and volcano in the [Virunga Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virunga_Mountains)

Rwanda has a [tropical highland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_climate) climate, with lower temperatures than are typical for equatorial countries because of its high elevation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2004-161] Precipitation usually ranges from 1,000 to 1,400 millimetres per year, depending on the area. Kigali, in the centre of the country, has a typical daily temperature range between 15 and 28 °C (59 and 82 °F), with little variation through the year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Meteorological_Organization-162] There are some temperature variations across the country; the mountainous west and north are generally cooler than the lower-lying east.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBest_Country_Reports2007-163] There are two rainy seasons in the year; the first runs from February to June and the second from September to December. These are separated by two [dry seasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dry_season): the major one from June to September, during which there is often no rain at all, and a shorter and less severe one from December to February.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200710-164] Rainfall varies geographically, with the west and northwest of the country receiving more precipitation annually than the east and southeast.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle20071-165] [Global warming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_warming) has caused a change in the pattern of the rainy seasons. According to a report by the Strategic Foresight Group, change in climate has reduced the number of rainy days experienced during a year, but has also caused an increase in frequency of torrential rains.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStrategic_Foresight_Group201329-166] Both changes have caused difficulty for farmers, decreasing their productivity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBucyensenge2014-167] Strategic Foresight also characterise Rwanda as a fast warming country, with an increase in average temperature of between 0.7 °C to 0.9 °C over fifty years.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStrategic_Foresight_Group201329-166]

### Biodiversity

Main article: [Wildlife of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_of_Rwanda)



[Volcanoes National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanoes_National_Park) is the home of the largest population of [mountain gorillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_gorillas) in the world.

In prehistoric times, [montane forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montane_forest) occupied one-third of the territory of present-day Rwanda. Naturally occurring vegetation is now mostly restricted to the [three national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_parks_of_Rwanda), with [terraced agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrace_\(agriculture\)) dominating the rest of the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth20063–4-168] [Nyungwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nyungwe_Forest), the largest remaining tract of forest, contains 200 species of tree as well as [orchids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchid) and [begonias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Begonia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200711-169] Vegetation in the [Volcanoes National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanoes_National_Park) is mostly [bamboo](39029.md) and moorland, with small areas of forest.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth20063–4-168] By contrast, Akagera has a [savanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savanna) ecosystem in which [acacia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia) dominates the flora. There are several rare or endangered plant species in Akagera, including *[Markhamia lutea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markhamia_lutea)* and *[Eulophia guineensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eulophia_guineensis)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEREMA_(Chapter_5)20093-170][^cite_note-171]

The greatest diversity of large mammals is found in the three national parks, which are designated conservation areas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGovernment_of_Rwanda_(II)-172] Akagera contains typical savanna animals such as giraffes and elephants,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERDB_(III)-173] while Volcanoes is home to an estimated one-third of the worldwide [mountain gorilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_gorilla) population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERDB_(I)2010-174] Nyungwe Forest boasts thirteen primate species including [common chimpanzees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_chimpanzee) and [*Ruwenzori colobus*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-and-white_colobus) arboreal monkeys; th*e Ruwenzori colobus* move in groups of up to 400 individuals, the largest troop size of any primate in Africa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006140-175]



Giraffe in Akagera National Park

Rwanda's population of [lions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion) was destroyed in the aftermath of the genocide of 1994, as national parks were turned into camps for displaced people and the remaining animals were poisoned by cattle herders. In June 2015, two South African parks donated seven lions to [Akagera National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akagera_National_Park), reestablishing a lion population in Rwanda.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith2015-176] The lions were held initially in a fenced-off area of the park, and then collared and released into the wild a month later.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThe_New_Times2015-177]

Eighteen endangered black rhinos were brought to Rwanda in 2017 from South Africa.[^cite_note-178] After positive results, five more black rhinos were delivered to Akagera National Park from zoos all over Europe in 2019.[^cite_note-179]

Similarly, the white rhino population is growing in Rwanda. In 2021, Rwanda received 30 white rhinos from South Africa with the goal of Akagera being a safe breeding ground for the near-threatened species.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181]

There are 670 [bird species in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Rwanda), with variation between the east and the west.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200715-182] Nyungwe Forest, in the west, has 280 recorded species, of which 26 are endemic to the Albertine Rift;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200715-182] endemic species include the *[Rwenzori turaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwenzori_turaco)* and [handsome spurfowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handsome_spurfowl).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWCS-183] Eastern Rwanda, by contrast, features savanna birds such as the [black-headed gonolek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-headed_gonolek) and those associated with swamps and lakes, including [storks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stork) and [cranes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crane_\(bird\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing200715-182]

Recent entomological work in the country has revealed a rich diversity of [praying mantises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Praying_mantises),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETedrow2015-184] including a new species *Dystacta tigrifrutex*, dubbed the "bush tiger mantis".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHowell2014-185]

Rwanda contains three terrestrial ecoregions: [Albertine Rift montane forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albertine_Rift_montane_forests), [Victoria Basin forest-savanna mosaic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Basin_forest-savanna_mosaic), and [Ruwenzori-Virunga montane moorlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruwenzori-Virunga_montane_moorlands).[^cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-186] The country had a 2019 [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index) mean score of 3.85/10, ranking it 139th globally out of 172 countries.[^cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-187]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Rwanda)

|  | This article needs to be **updated**. The reason given is: **This section does not reflect recent developments in Rwanda's economy (Economy of Rwanda page covers it slightly better).**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(August 2025)* |
| --- | --- |

Rwanda's economy suffered heavily during the 1994 genocide, with widespread loss of life, failure to maintain infrastructure, looting, and neglect of important cash crops. This caused a large drop in GDP and heavily damaged the country's ability to attract private and external investment. However, in the 2000s Rwanda witnessed an economic boom which improved the standard of living for many Rwandans.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77][^cite_note-imf_data-188] The [economy of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Rwanda) has expanded consistently since the [1994 genocide](676659.md), with real GDP growth of 8.2% in 2023, 8.9% in 2024, and 9.4% in 2025; as reported by the [National Institute of Statistics of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda).[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-\:4-190] Growth is projected to be 7.5% in 2026, and the government is actively pursuing policies to encourage industrial growth, such as the "Made In Rwanda".[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193]

As of the most recent survey in 2024, 30.5% of the population are affected by [multidimensional poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multidimensional_Poverty_Index) with 27.4% of the population under the national poverty line, a significant drop from the monetary poverty of 60.3% in 2000.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197] The UN classification for extreme poverty, or living below US\$3 per day, estimates 38.5% of individuals fall in this category.[^cite_note-198] In 2024, the country was classified by the UN as one of forty-four [least developed countries (LDC)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Least_developed_countries), despite recent progress in growth.[^cite_note-199] Rwanda's [GNI per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GNI_\(nominal\)_per_capita) is US\$3,620, and represents an 11.04% growth from 2023.[^cite_note-200]

Rwanda's economy is mainly driven by the [service sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector), which has seen frequent growth. Service sector production constitutes 52% of total GDP, followed by industry (22%) and agriculture (20%).[^cite_note-\:4-190] Agriculture, including for [subsistence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsistence_agriculture) and [cash crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cash_crop), have a much higher share of employment than the other sectors (64.5%). More than two thirds of households in the country engage in agriculture, primarily undertaking crop farming and livestock rearing.[^cite_note-201]

### Trade

In 2024 top export markets include [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), [Democratic Republic of the Congo](76762.md), [China](5405.md), [South Sudan](32350676.md) and the [United Kingdom](31717.md).[^cite_note-\:6-202] Total merchandise exports, which exclude services, increased nearly 30% from 2023 to 2024.[^cite_note-\:6-202] Primary exports are agriculture products, including [horticulture products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horticulture), [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee), tea and processed agriculture goods.[^cite_note-203] Other major exports include precious stones and metal and ores, oil and manufactured goods ([agro-processing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_processing), cement, furniture, textiles and plastic pipes).[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-\:3-205][^cite_note-\:0-206][^cite_note-207] Rwanda imports from its main partners, including [China](5405.md), [India](14533.md), [South Korea](27019.md) and [Germany](11867.md). Local importers within Africa make up nearly 20% of total imports and include [Uganda](31816.md), [Tanzania](30118.md), [Kenya](188171.md), and [Egypt](8087628.md).[^cite_note-\:5-208] Key imports include oil and petroleum products, sugar, telephones and electronics, and packaged healthcare supplies.[^cite_note-\:5-208]

### Agriculture

Agriculture is prominent in Rwanda and approximately 59% of the land is used for agricultural processes.[^cite_note-209]

Coffee in Kigali

Coffee is Rwanda's largest cash crop due to profitability, although far exceeded in banana production

[^cite_note-Rwanda_production_in_2019,_by_FAO-210]

Crops grown in the country include [matoke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matoke) (green bananas), which occupy more than a third of the country's farmland,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa-211] [potatoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potato), [beans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bean), [sweet potatoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweet_potato), [cassava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassava), [wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheat) and [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOur_Africa-211] Coffee and tea are the major cash crops for export, with the high altitudes, steep slopes and volcanic soils providing favourable conditions. Reports have established that more than 400,000 Rwandans make their living from coffee plantation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETumwebaze2016-212] Reliance on agricultural exports makes Rwanda vulnerable to shifts in their prices.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWTO2004-213] Animals raised in Rwanda include cows, goats, sheep, pigs, chicken, and rabbits, with geographical variation in the numbers of each.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINAGRI2006-214] Production systems are mostly traditional, although there are a few intensive dairy farms around Kigali.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINAGRI2006-214] Shortages of land and water, insufficient and poor-quality feed, and regular disease epidemics with insufficient veterinary services are major constraints that restrict output. Fishing takes place on the country's lakes, but stocks are very depleted, and live fish are being imported in an attempt to revive the industry.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENamata2008-215]

To promote sustainable practices, the government of Rwanda partnered with the Howard G. Buffett Foundation to establish the [Rwanda Institute for Conservation Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_Institute_for_Conservation_Agriculture) (RICA) in 2019. Located in [Bugesera District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bugesera_District), the institution provides hands-on training in [conservation agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservation_agriculture) and "[One Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Health)" principles.[^cite_note-216]

### Industry and manufacturing

The industrial sector is currently experiencing growth, contributing 21.5% of GDP in 2014.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFAO_/_WFP1997-217] Products manufactured include cement, agricultural products, small-scale beverages, soap, furniture, shoes, plastic goods, textiles and cigarettes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77] Rwanda's mining industry is an important contributor, generating US\$93 million in 2008.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMukaaya2009-218] Minerals mined include [cassiterite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassiterite), [wolframite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolframite), gold, and [coltan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coltan), which is used in the manufacture of electronic and communication devices such as mobile phones.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMukaaya2009-218][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDelawala2001-219]

Rwanda's [service sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_sector) suffered during the [late-2000s recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late-2000s_recession) as bank lending, foreign aid projects and investment were reduced.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENantaba2010-220] The sector rebounded in 2010, becoming the country's largest sector by economic output and contributing 43.6% of the country's GDP.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77] Key tertiary contributors include banking and finance, wholesale and retail trade, hotels and restaurants, transport, storage, communication, insurance, real estate, business services and public administration including education and health.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENantaba2010-220]

Rwanda was ranked 104th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222]

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Rwanda)

Tourism is one of Rwanda's fastest-growing economic sectors and remains the country's leading foreign exchange earner. In 2023, Rwanda recorded over 1.4 million international visitors, generating about US\$620 million in revenue, according to the [Rwanda Development Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_Development_Board) (RDB).[^cite_note-223] The [World Travel & Tourism Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Travel_and_Tourism_Council) reported that in 2024, the sector supported nearly 386,000 jobs in Rwanda and contributed over 10% of GDP.[^cite_note-224] The government has explicitly created an agenda for high cost, low traffic luxury tourism, emphasizing high end lodges and experiences within a natural setting.[^cite_note-\:7-225] The tourism was greatly impacted by the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), as well as the [Ebola outbreak in 2026](83191582.md) in neighboring Uganda and DRC.[^cite_note-226]

A major draw for visitors is [mountain gorilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_gorilla) tracking in [Volcanoes National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanoes_National_Park), one of only three places worldwide where mountain gorillas can be seen in the wild.[^cite_note-227] A single mountain gorilla permits cost US\$1,500 for foreigners and are highly restricted to a few number of individuals per day.[^cite_note-\:7-225] Other attractions include [Nyungwe Forest National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nyungwe_Forest_National_Park), known for its chimpanzees and canopy walkway,[^cite_note-228] the resorts of [Lake Kivu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Kivu), and [Akagera National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akagera_National_Park); a savanna reserve in the east home to elephants, lions, and giraffes.

The government, through the RDB, has promoted tourism as a pillar of economic growth.[^cite_note-229] Visitor numbers have increased steadily since the early 2000s, with international arrivals often linked to conferences in Kigali as well as ecotourism.[^cite_note-230] In 2018, Rwanda launched the *Visit Rwanda* campaign, including sponsorship deals with European football clubs such as [Arsenal F.C.](2174.md), [Paris Saint-Germain F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Saint-Germain_F.C.), and [Atlético Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atl%C3%A9tico_Madrid), to raise the country's profile as a travel destination.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233]

In 2025, Visit Rwanda expanded its sports marketing into the United States, entering multi-year agreements with the [Los Angeles Clippers](72892.md) (NBA) and the [Los Angeles Rams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Rams) (NFL). As part of the Clippers partnership, Visit Rwanda became the exclusive jersey patch sponsor and the official coffee sponsor of the team's Intuit Dome arena.[^cite_note-234] In the Rams deal, Visit Rwanda is an official international tourism sponsor of the team, SoFi Stadium, and Hollywood Park, and will secure prominent advertising and entitlement placements within the stadium complex.[^cite_note-235]

Tourism in Rwanda has been seen as a model for other countries in [East Africa](260987.md), as it focuses on sustainability and [conservation](45441.md).[^cite_note-236] The government has a program in which it directly shares a portion of annual tourism revenues, 5% beginning in 2005, and increasing to 10% in 2017, with the local communities around the park. Despite marketing, there is still debate on whether the sector is improving the conditions locally. In a survey, those working within the tourism industry did not self report the same positive perception of Rwanda as visitors had, particularly on politics and safety.[^cite_note-237] Similarly, a review conducted fifteen years from the deployment of the scheme found about 57% of community members near parks felt satisfied with the scheme, while 20% remained dissatisfied. Vulnerable communities, those not a part of a formal [cooperative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperative) or not involved in local governance reported lowest levels of satisfaction with the program.[^cite_note-238]

### Media and communications

Main articles: [Telecommunications in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Rwanda), [Media of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Rwanda), and [Censorship in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship_in_Rwanda)

The largest radio and television stations are state-run, and the majority of newspapers are owned by the government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2015a-239] Most Rwandans have access to radio; during the [1994 genocide](676659.md), the radio station [Radio Télévision Libre des Mille Collines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_T%C3%A9l%C3%A9vision_Libre_des_Mille_Collines) broadcast across the country, and helped to fuel the killings through anti-Tutsi propaganda.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2015a-239] As of 2015, the state-run [Radio Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Rwanda) was the largest station and the main source of news throughout the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2015a-239] Television access was limited, with most homes not having their own set.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGasore2014-240] The government rolled out [digital television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_television) in 2014, and a year later there were seven national stations operating, up from just one in the pre-2014 analogue era.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOpobo2015-241] The press is tightly restricted, and newspapers routinely self-censor to avoid government reprisals.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2015a-239] Nonetheless, publications in Kinyarwanda, English, and French critical of the government are widely available in Kigali. Restrictions were increased in the run-up to the Rwandan presidential election of 2010, with two independent newspapers, *Umuseso* and *Umuvugizi*, being suspended for six months by the High Media Council.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReporters_Without_Borders2010-242]

The country's oldest telecommunications group, [Rwandatel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandatel), went into liquidation in 2011, having been 80% owned by Libyan company LAP Green.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMugisha2013-243] The company was acquired in 2013 by Liquid Telecom,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESouthwood2013-244] a company providing telecommunications and [fibre-optic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fibre-optic) networks across eastern and southern Africa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMugwe2013-245] In 2015, Liquid Telecom provided [landline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landline) service to 30,968 subscribers, with mobile operator [MTN Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTN_Rwanda) serving an additional 15,497 fixed line subscribers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20156-246] Landlines are mostly used by government institutions, banks, [NGOs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Government_Organisation) and embassies, with private subscription levels low.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMajyambere2010-247] In 2015, [mobile phone](19644137.md) penetration in the country was 72.6%,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20155-248] up from 41.6% in 2011.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20113-249] MTN Rwanda is the leading provider, with 3,957,986 subscribers, followed by [Tigo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tigo) with 2,887,328, and [Bharti Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharti_Airtel) with 1,336,679.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20156-246] Rwandatel has also previously operated a mobile phone network, but the industry regulator revoked its licence in April 2011, following the company's failure to meet agreed investment commitments.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButera2011-250] Internet penetration is low but rising rapidly; in 2015 there were 12.8 internet users per 100 people,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERURA20155-248] up from 2.1 in 2007.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(II)-251] In 2011, a 2,300-kilometre (1,400 mi) fibre-optic telecommunications network was completed, intended to provide broadband services and facilitate electronic commerce.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReuters2011-252] This network is connected to [SEACOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEACOM_\(African_cable_system\)), a [submarine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine_communications_cable) fibre-optic cable connecting communication carriers in southern and eastern Africa. Within Rwanda the cables run along major roads, linking towns around the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReuters2011-252] Mobile provider MTN also runs a [wireless internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wireless_internet) service accessible in most areas of Kigali via pre-paid subscription.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButera2010-253] As of 2024, the largest [internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet) providers are MTN and Airtel. MTN Rwanda has shown impressive growth in its subscriber base. As of the first quarter of 2024, MTN Rwanda had approximately 7.4 million mobile subscribers. (Additionally, their Mobile Money platform, MoMo, had around 5.1 million users), compared to Airtel Rwanda's 5,792,046 active mobile-cellular telephone subscriptions. In October 2019, [Mara Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mara_Corporation) launched the first African-made smartphone in Rwanda.[^cite_note-254] Following its launch in 2024, Airtel Rwanda, in cooperation with the Rwandan government, released the most affordable smartphone in the world, the Airtel Imagine 4G. The phone was introduced as part of the ConnectRwanda 2.0 initiative. It costs only 20,000 RWF (approximately US\$14.49).

### Infrastructure

Main articles: [Transport in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Rwanda), [Energy in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Rwanda), and [Water supply and sanitation in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Rwanda)

#### Water supply and sanitation

Photograph depicting one adult and five children filling jerrycans at a rural metal water pump with concrete base, at the bottom of a steep rocky hillside

Rural water pump

The Rwandan government prioritised funding of water supply development during the 2000s, significantly increasing its share of the national budget.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIDA2009-255] This funding, along with donor support, caused a rapid increase in access to safe water. In 2015, 74% of the population had access to safe water,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015-256] up from about 55% in 2005;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIDA2009-255] the government initially set a target to increase this to 100% by 2017.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015-256]

The country's water infrastructure consists of urban and rural systems that deliver water to the public, mainly through standpipes in rural areas and private connections in urban areas. In areas not served by these systems, hand pumps and managed springs are used.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINECOFIN200225–26-257] Despite rainfall exceeding 750 millimetres (30 in) annually in most of the country,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerryLewisWilliams1990533-258] little use is made of [rainwater harvesting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainwater_harvesting), and residents are forced to use water very sparingly relative to usage in other African countries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUmutesi2015-256]

Access to [sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Rwanda) historically lagged behind water access; the United Nations estimated that in 2006, only 34% of urban and 20% of rural dwellers had access to [improved sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improved_sanitation).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083-259] By 2022, overall access to improved sanitation had increased significantly to 92% of the total population (95% urban and 91% rural).[^cite_note-260] Government policy measures to improve sanitation have traditionally focused heavily on urban areas,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083-259] and Kigali has been recognized as one of the cleanest cities in Africa.[^cite_note-261] However, a majority of the population across both rural and urban areas continues to rely on public shared [pit latrines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_latrine).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(I)20083-259]

#### Energy



Electricity generation in Rwanda in terawatt-hours

Until the early 2000s, Rwanda's electricity supply was generated almost entirely from [hydroelectric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectric) sources; power stations on Lakes [Burera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Burera) and [Ruhondo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ruhondo) provided 90% of the country's electricity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20113-262] A combination of below-average rainfall and human activity, including the draining of the [Rugezi wetlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugezi_wetlands?action=edit\&redlink=1) for cultivation and grazing, caused the two lakes' water levels to fall from 1990 onward; by 2004, levels were reduced by 50%, leading to a sharp drop in output from the power stations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115-263] This, coupled with increased demand as the economy grew, precipitated a shortfall in 2004 and widespread [loadshedding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loadshedding).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115-263]

As an emergency measure, the government installed [diesel generators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_generator) north of Kigali; by 2006 these were providing 56% of the country's costly electricity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Resources_Report20115-263] The government later enacted a number of measures to alleviate this problem, including rehabilitating the Rugezi wetlands and investing in a scheme to extract methane gas from Lake Kivu to increase power generation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAfDB2011-264] Only 18% of the population had access to electricity in 2012, though this had risen from 10.8% in 2009.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(XIII)-265] The government's Economic Development and Poverty Reduction Strategy for 2013–18 aimed to scale up infrastructure to bring household electricity access to 70% by 2017.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaringanireMalikBanerjee20141-266]

#### Transport

The government has increased investment in the [transport infrastructure of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Rwanda) since the 1994 genocide, with aid from the United States, [European Union](9317.md), Japan, and other international donors. The transport system consists primarily of a road network, featuring paved roads connecting Kigali to most other major cities and towns.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAfDBOECD_Development_Centre2006439-267] Rwanda is linked by road to neighbor nations in the East African Community—namely Uganda, Tanzania, Burundi, and [Kenya](188171.md)—as well as to the eastern Congolese cities of [Goma](1210483.md) and [Bukavu](448570.md). The country's most critical trade corridor is the road to the port of [Mombasa](200152.md) via [Kampala](56689.md) and [Nairobi](21482.md), known as the [Northern Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Corridor).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETancott2014-268]

The principal form of domestic public transport is the [minibus](1094925.md), accounting for more than half of all passenger-carrying capacity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334-269] Some minibuses, particularly within Kigali,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201367-270] operate unscheduled services under a [shared taxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shared_taxi) system,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201332-271] while others run on fixed schedules offering express routes between major cities. A smaller number of larger buses operate scheduled routes nationwide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334-269] For private hire, the dominant vehicle is the [motorcycle taxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorcycle_taxi); in 2013, there were 9,609 registered motorcycle taxis in Rwanda, compared with just 579 standard [taxicabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxicab).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201334-269] Long-distance [coach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coach_\(vehicle\)) services are also available to destinations in neighboring countries.

Aviation is centered at [Kigali International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kigali_International_Airport), which serves several international destinations; the busiest routes are those to [Nairobi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JKIA) and [Entebbe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entebbe_International_Airport).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECentre_For_Aviation2014-272] A single domestic route connects Kigali to [Kamembe Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamembe_Airport) near [Cyangugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyangugu).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETumwebaze2015-273] In 2017, construction began on the [Bugesera International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bugesera_International_Airport) south of Kigali, designed to become the country's largest facility upon completion to complement the existing infrastructure.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA2017-274] The national carrier is [RwandAir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RwandAir), and the country is additionally served by several foreign airlines.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECentre_For_Aviation2014-272]

As of the mid-2010s, the country had no operational railways, though a project was initiated in conjunction with Burundi and Tanzania to extend the Tanzanian [Central Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Line_\(Tanzania\)) into Rwanda via a [public private partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_private_partnership).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESenelwa2015-275] There is no public water transport network across the port cities on Lake Kivu, although a limited private service exists and the government has initiated a development programme for full commercial service.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201343-276] The [Ministry of Infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Infrastructure_\(Rwanda\)) has also investigated the feasibility of linking Rwanda to [Lake Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Victoria) via shipping lanes on the [Akagera River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akagera_River).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMININFRA201343-276]

### Macroeconomics

See also: [National Bank of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Rwanda) and [Economy of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Rwanda)

Monetary policy and management of government finances is controlled by the central [National Bank of Rwanda (BNR)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Rwanda). The currency used is the [Rwandan franc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_franc), created in 1964.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278] In August 2025, the exchange rate was 1445 francs to one US dollar. Rwanda joined the [East African Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Community) in 2007, and has ratified a plan for [monetary union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monetary_union) amongst the seven member nations,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAsiimwe2014-279] with an earlier policy goal of creating a unified [East African shilling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_shilling#Second_East_African_shilling).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELavelle2008-280]

#### Financial services

The financial sector in Rwanda is the third-largest contributor to the service sector and is supervised by the BNR. As of March 2025, the sector's total assets stood at FRW 13.6 trillion (approx. US\$9.4 billion), representing a year-on-year growth of 23.2%.[^cite_note-BNR-FSC-May2025-281]

Rwanda has achieved near-universal financial inclusion, largely driven by mobile technology. According to the 2024 FinScope survey, 96% of the adult population is [financially included](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_inclusion), with the majority relying on non-bank [mobile financial services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile_commerce).[^cite_note-FinScope2024-282]

[Mobile money platforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile_banking), operated primarily by [MTN Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTN_Rwanda) and [Airtel Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airtel_Uganda), have effectively replaced cash for daily economic activity.
