In 2024, the total value of mobile money transactions reached Rwf 21.0 trillion, exceeding the country's nominal GDP (Rwf 18.8 trillion).[^cite_note-MTN-FY2024-283] This high monetary velocity is supported by the government's "Cashless Rwanda" policy and the "eKash" interoperability system, which allows seamless transfers between rival [telecom operators](33094374.md) and [commercial banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_bank).[^cite_note-NewTimesEKash-284]

#### Capital markets

The [Rwanda Stock Exchange (RSE)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_Stock_Exchange_\(RSE\)?action=edit\&redlink=1) is the principal stock exchange with a market capitalization of approximately FRW 4.6 trillion (US\$3.1 billion) as of December 2025.[^cite_note-285] In 2024, the market expanded into sustainable finance with the listing the first *green bonds* and environmental-outcome linked bonds. Parallel to equities, the [East Africa Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Africa_Exchange) (EAX) is a regional agricultural commodities exchange based in Kigali, which operates a warehouse receipt system for stored agricultural commodities, including the use of electronic warehouse receipts in commodity trading.[^cite_note-286]

The sector is anchored by the [Kigali International Financial Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kigali_International_Financial_Centre) (KIFC), a government initiative launched in 2020 to position Rwanda as a pan-African financial hub. Managed by [Rwanda Finance Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_Finance_Limited?action=edit\&redlink=1), KIFC provides a legal and tax framework designed to attract investment funds, family offices, and fintech ventures.[^cite_note-KIFCAbout-287]

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Rwanda) and [Youth in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_in_Rwanda)

|  | This article needs to be **updated**. The reason given is: **This section reflects more 1990s–2000s Rwanda rather than 30 years later developments**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(August 2025)* |
| --- | --- |

As of 2025, Rwanda's estimated population is around 14.6 million, although estimates vary.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289] A decade earlier, in 2015, the estimated population was 11,262,564 (according to the [National Institute of Statistics of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda)), and the 2012 census recorded a population of 10,515,973.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2015-290][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda20143-291] The population is young: as of 2024–2025, estimates of the median age ranged from 19.9 to 20.8, with 42.9% of the population under the age of 15, and 54.7% between 15 and 64.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda20148-293][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77] In 2015, according to the CIA *[World Factbook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Factbook)*, the annual birth rate was estimated at 40.2 births per 1,000 inhabitants, and the death rate at 14.9.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77] The current life expectancy is between 68.2 and 70.54 years (71 years for females and 66 years for males), placing it 156th out of 224 countries and territories.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(III)2011-294] The overall sex ratio of the country is 95.9 males per 100 females.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77]

Photograph depicting seven rural children, with a straw house and farmland in the background, taken in the Volcanoes National Park in 2005

Rural children

At 445 inhabitants per square kilometre (1,150/sq mi),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda2015-290] Rwanda's population density is amongst the highest in Africa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBanda2015-295] Historians such as [Gérard Prunier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%A9rard_Prunier) believe that the 1994 genocide can be partly attributed to the population density.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19954-60] The population is predominantly rural, with a few large towns; dwellings are evenly spread throughout the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStraus2013215-296] The only sparsely populated area of the country is the savanna land in the former province of [Umutara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umutara_Province) and Akagera National Park in the east.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStreissguth200711-297] Kigali is the largest city, with a population of around one million.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKigali_City-298] Its rapidly increasing population challenges its infrastructural development.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPercivalHomer-Dixon1995-299][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEREMA_(Chapter_2)2009-300] According to the 2012 census, the second largest city is [Gisenyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisenyi), which lies adjacent to [Lake Kivu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Kivu) and the Congolese city of [Goma](1210483.md), and has a population of 126,000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECity_Population2012-301] Other major towns include [Ruhengeri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruhengeri), [Butare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butare), and [Muhanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhanga), all with populations below 100,000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECity_Population2012-301] The urban population rose from 6% of the population in 1990,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPercivalHomer-Dixon1995-299] to 16.6% in 2006;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201229-302] by 2011, however, the proportion had dropped slightly, to 14.8%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201229-302]

Rwanda has been a unified state since pre-colonial times,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAppiahGates2010450-48] and the population is drawn from just one cultural and linguistic group, the [Banyarwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banyarwanda);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200252-303] this contrasts with most modern African states, whose borders were drawn by [colonial powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonisation_of_Africa) and did not correspond to ethnic boundaries or pre-colonial kingdoms.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoyd19791-304] Within the Banyarwanda people, there are three separate groups, the Hutu, Tutsi and Twa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier19955-305] The CIA *World Factbook* gives estimates that the Hutu made up 84% of the population in 2009, the Tutsi 15% and Twa 1%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECIA_(I)-77] The Twa are a pygmy people who descend from Rwanda's earliest inhabitants, but scholars do not agree on the origins of and differences between the Hutu and Tutsi.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200246–47-306] Anthropologist [Jean Hiernaux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Hiernaux?action=edit\&redlink=1) contends that the Tutsi are a separate race, with a tendency towards "long and narrow heads, faces and noses";[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMamdani200247-307] others, such as Villia Jefremovas, believe there is no discernible physical difference and the categories were not historically rigid.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJefremovas1995-308] In precolonial Rwanda the Tutsi were the ruling class, from whom the kings and the majority of chiefs were derived, while the Hutu were agriculturalists.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrunier199511–12-309] The current [government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Rwanda) discourages the Hutu/Tutsi/Twa distinction, and has removed such classification from identity cards.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeman2010-310] The 2002 census was the first since 1933[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKiwuwa201271-311] which did not categorise Rwandan population into the three groups.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAgence_France-Presse2002-312]

### Education

Main article: [Education in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Rwanda)



Children in a Rwandan primary school, using laptops supplied by the [One Laptop Per Child](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Laptop_Per_Child) programme

Prior to 2012, the Rwandan government provided nine years of free basic education in state-run schools, consisting of six years of primary and three years of lower secondary education (known as the *Tronc Commun*).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINEDUC20102-314] In 2012, this policy was expanded to cover twelve years of free basic education, including upper secondary.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015935-315]

A 2015 study noted that while primary school enrollment was nearly universal, completion rates remained low and repetition rates high.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015931-316] Although schooling is officially fee free, parents are expected to contribute to certain costs such as school materials, teacher support, and infrastructure maintenance. The government maintains that these contributions should not prevent any child from attending school.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliamsAbbottMupenzi2015935-315]

There are also many private and church affiliated schools that follow the national curriculum but charge tuition fees.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200627-317] From 1994 until 2009, secondary education was offered in either French or English. Following Rwanda's accession to the [East African Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Community) and the [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations), English became the main medium of instruction from 2009 onward.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcGreal2009-318]

The country has several institutions of higher learning. In 2013, the government established the [University of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Rwanda) (UR) through the merger of the [National University of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_University_of_Rwanda) and other public higher education institutions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoenig2014-319][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMacGregor2014-320][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERutayisire2013-321] In 2023, the [gross enrollment ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_enrollment_ratio) for tertiary education was 8.89%, up from 3.6% in 2006.[^cite_note-322] Rwanda's adult literacy rate, defined as the share of those aged 15 and above who can read and write, rose from 38% in 1978 to 58% in 1991, 71% in 2009, and 78.8% in 2022.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(I)-323][^cite_note-324]

### Health

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(October 2024)* |
| --- | --- |

Main articles: [Healthcare in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Rwanda) and [Health in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Rwanda)

Photograph depicting a hospital building, with Rwandan flag, viewed from the entrance pathway

[Butaro Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butaro_Hospital) at [Burera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burera), Northern Province&#x20;

The quality of healthcare in Rwanda has historically been very low, both before and immediately after the 1994 genocide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDrobacNaughton2014-325] In 1998, more than one in five children died before their fifth birthday,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(IV)-326] often from [malaria](20423.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBowdler2010-327]

President Kagame has made healthcare one of the priorities for the [Vision 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vision_2020_\(Rwanda\)) development programme,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEvans2014-328] boosting spending on health care to 6.5% of the country's [gross domestic product](12594.md) in 2013,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(V)-329] compared with 1.9% in 1996.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VI)-330] The government has devolved the financing and management of healthcare to local communities, through a system of health insurance providers called *mutuelles de santé*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWHO2008-331] The *mutuelles* were piloted in 1999, and were made available nationwide by the mid-2000s, with the assistance of international development partners.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWHO2008-331] Premiums under the scheme were initially US\$2 per annum; since 2011 the rate has varied on a sliding scale, with the poorest paying nothing, and maximum premiums rising to US\$8 per adult.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERosenberg2012-332] As of 2014, more than 90% of the population was covered by the scheme.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(II)2014-333] The government has also set up training institutes including the Kigali Health Institute (KHI), which was established in 1997[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIMF200034-334] and is now part of the [University of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Rwanda). In 2005, President Kagame also launched a programme known as *The Presidents' Malaria Initiative*.[^cite_note-335] This initiative aimed to help get the most necessary materials for prevention of malaria to the most rural areas of Rwanda, such as mosquito nets and medication.



Historical development of life expectancy in Rwanda, showing an extremely sharp drop in [1994](676659.md).

In recent years Rwanda has seen improvement on a number of key health indicators. Between 2005 and 2013, life expectancy increased from 55.2 to 64.0,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VII)-336] under-5 mortality decreased from 106.4 to 52.0 per 1,000 live births,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(VIII)-337] and incidence of [tuberculosis](30653.md) has dropped from 101 to 69 per 100,000 people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank_(IX)-338] The country's progress in healthcare has been cited by the international media and charities. *[The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic)* devoted an article to "Rwanda's Historic Health Recovery".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEmery2013-339] [Partners In Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partners_In_Health) described the health gains "among the most dramatic the world has seen in the last 50 years".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERosenberg2012-332]

Despite these improvements, however, the country's health profile remains dominated by communicable diseases,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWHO2015-340] and the [United States Agency for International Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Agency_for_International_Development) has described "significant health challenges",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015-341] including the rate of maternal mortality, which it describes as "unacceptably high",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015-341] as well as the ongoing [HIV/AIDS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV/AIDS) epidemic.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUSAID_(III)2015-341] According to the American [Centers for Disease Control and Prevention](6811.md), travellers to Rwanda are highly recommended to take preventive malaria medication as well as make sure they are up to date with vaccines such as yellow fever.[^cite_note-342]

Rwanda also has a shortage of medical professionals, with only 0.84 physicians, nurses, and midwives per 1,000 residents.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPartners_In_Health2013-343] The [United Nations Development Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme) (UNDP) is monitoring the country's health progress towards [Millennium Development Goals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Development_Goals) 4–6, which relate to healthcare. A mid-2015 UNDP report noted that the country was not on target to meet goal 4 on infant mortality, despite it having "fallen dramatically";[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(II)2015-344] the country is "making good progress" towards goal 5, which is to reduce by three quarters the maternal mortality ratio,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(III)2015-345] while goal 6 is not yet met as [HIV prevalence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV/AIDS_in_Rwanda) has not started falling.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNDP_(IV)2015-346]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Rwanda)

Photograph depicting the Marian sanctuary of Kibeho, a place of the recognized Marian apparitions occurred in Africa

Catholic shrine of [Our Lady of Kibeho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Kibeho)

The largest faith in Rwanda is [Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholicism), but there have been significant changes in the nation's religious demographics since the genocide, with many conversions to [evangelical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical) Christianity, and, to a lesser degree, [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Rwanda).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalkerApril_2004-347] According to the 2012 census, Catholic Christians represented 43.7% of the population, Protestants (excluding [Seventh-day Adventists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventists)) 37.7%, Seventh-day Adventists 11.8%, and Muslims 2.0%; 0.2% claimed no religious beliefs and 1.3% did not state a religion.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENational_Institute_of_Statistics_of_Rwanda201417-348] Traditional religion, despite officially being followed by only 0.1% of the population, retains an influence. Many Rwandans view the [Christian God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/God_in_Christianity) as synonymous with the traditional Rwandan God *[Imana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imana)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWireduAbrahamIreleMenkiti2006236–237-349]

### Languages

Main articles: [Languages of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Rwanda) and [Language policy in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Language_policy_in_Rwanda)

The country's principal and national language is [Kinyarwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinyarwanda), which is spoken by almost the entire population (about 98%).[^cite_note-350] The inhabitants of the islands on Lake Kivu, including the island of Nkombo and the coastal areas of the western province of Rwanda, speak [Mashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi_language), a Congolese language widely spoken in the Democratic Republic of Congo. This is due to the ancient belonging of these territories to the former [Kingdom of Bushi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bushi_\(region\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENakayima2010-351]

During the colonial era, [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language) was the first European language introduced to Rwanda, though it was never widely used or taught. After 1916, [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language) became dominant under Belgian administration and was used in government and education. The 1962 Constitution made French an official language alongside Kinyarwanda.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUniversité_Laval2010-352][^cite_note-\:1-353] [Dutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_language) was also used in limited administrative contexts during the Belgian period.

The return of English-speaking Rwandan refugees from [Uganda](31816.md), [Kenya](188171.md), and [Tanzania](30118.md) in the 1990s[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUniversité_Laval2010-352] reshaped the country's linguistic landscape and accelerated the shift toward English.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESamuelsonFreedman2010-354] As Rwanda deepened its ties with the [East African Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Community) and joined the [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations), English became increasingly important in education, business, and administration. In 2008, the government changed the medium of instruction in schools from French to English to strengthen regional integration.[^cite_note-355] Since then, the starting point of English instruction in schools has been adjusted several times.[^cite_note-356] While the national curriculum is in English, private schools offering international curricula may still opt to teach in French.[^cite_note-\:2-357]

As a member of the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations), Rwanda officially adheres to [Commonwealth English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_English) ([British English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_English)) conventions in education and government administration;[^cite_note-REB_Syllabus-358] however, the variety used locally is sometimes referred to as [Rwandan English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_English)[^cite_note-WEGATE-359] and is described as an [African English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_English) variety shaped by Rwanda's multilingual environment, particularly contact with [Kinyarwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinyarwanda).[^cite_note-WEGATE-359] However, there is no expert linguistic consensus on whether a variety distinct and consistent enough to be labelled 'Rwandan English' yet exists.[^cite_note-Spowage2025-360]

Today, [Kinyarwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinyarwanda), [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language), [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language), and [Swahili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swahili_language) are all official languages.[^cite_note-361] Kinyarwanda serves as the national and unifying language, while English is now the primary language of government, education, and business. French remains in limited use in diplomacy and cultural contexts, and [Swahili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swahili_language), the [lingua franca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingua_franca) of the [East African Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Community),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETabaro2015-362] is taught in schools and used in regional trade. Swahili was introduced as a compulsory subject in secondary schools in 2015.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETabaro2015-362]

According to the 2012 Census of Rwanda, literacy among residents aged 15 and above was 67.7% in Kinyarwanda, 14.7% in English, and 11.4% in French.[^cite_note-STATS2012-363] A decade later, the 2022 Census showed notable shifts: literacy in Kinyarwanda had risen to 78.3%, English to 21.2%, French had decreased to 8.2%, and Swahili reached 4.0%.[^cite_note-UN2022-364] These results reflect the steady growth of English and Swahili literacy and a continuing decline in the proportion of French-literate speakers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESamuelsonFreedman2010-354] Kinyarwanda remains the mother tongue of nearly all Rwandans, while English and Swahili use continue to expand, particularly in urban areas and schools.[^cite_note-365]<sup>: 73 </sup> As in many postcolonial societies, proficiency in European languages is closely linked to education, migration background, and social class.[^cite_note-\:1-353][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESamuelsonFreedman2010-354]

### Human rights

|  | This section **may be [unbalanced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:DUE) towards certain viewpoints**. Please [improve the article](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rwanda\&action=edit) or discuss the issue on the [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:Rwanda). *(March 2025)* |
| --- | --- |

See also: [Human rights in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Rwanda) and [LGBT rights in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Rwanda)

Homosexuality is generally considered a [taboo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taboo) topic, and there is no significant public discussion of this issue in any region of the country. Some lesbian, gay, bisexual and transgender (LGBT) Rwandans have reported being harassed and blackmailed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGmünder20071216-366][^cite_note-Spartacus_International_Gay_Guide_2007-367][^cite_note-globalgayz-368] Same-sex sexual activity is not specifically illegal in Rwanda. Some cabinet-level government officials have expressed support for the rights of LGBT people;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEU.S._Department_of_State2016-369] however, no special legislative protections are afforded to LGBT people,[^cite_note-Spartacus_International_Gay_Guide_2007-367] who may be arrested by the police under various laws dealing with public order and morality.[^cite_note-globalgayz-368] [Same-sex marriages](92656.md) are not recognized by the state, as the [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Rwanda) provides that "\[o]nly civil monogamous marriage between a man and a woman is recognized".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECJCR2003article_26-370]

Since 2006, Human Rights Watch has documented that Rwandan authorities round up and detain [street children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Street_children), street vendors, sex workers, homeless people, and beggars. They have also documented the use of torture in safe houses and other facilities, such as Kami military camp, Kwa Gacinya and [Gikondo prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gikondo_prison?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-hrw-371]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Rwanda)

The culture of Rwanda is varied. Unlike many other countries in Africa, Rwanda has been a unified state since precolonial times, populated by the [Banyarwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banyarwanda) people who share a [single language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinyarwanda) and cultural heritage.

### Arts

See also: [Art of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_of_Rwanda), [Architecture of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Rwanda), and [Music of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Rwanda)



Visual art depicting traditional milk churning

[Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Rwanda) and dance are an integral part of Rwandan ceremonies, festivals, social gatherings and storytelling. The most famous traditional dance is a highly choreographed routine consisting of three components: the *[umushagiriro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umushagiriro?action=edit\&redlink=1)*, or cow dance, performed by women;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERwanda_Development_Gateway-372] the *[intore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intore)*, or dance of heroes, performed by men;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERwanda_Development_Gateway-372] and the drumming, also traditionally performed by men, on drums known as *ingoma*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERMCA-373] The best-known dance group is the [National Ballet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Ballet_of_Rwanda). It was established by President Habyarimana in 1974, and performs nationally and internationally.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggs2004-374] Traditionally, music is transmitted orally, with styles varying between the social groups. Drums are of great importance; the royal drummers enjoyed high status within the court of the King (*Mwami*).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle2007135–136-375] Drummers play together in groups of varying sizes, usually between seven and nine in number.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle2007139-376] The country has a growing popular music industry, influenced by East African, Congolese, and American music. The most popular genre is [hip hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip_hop), with a blend of [dancehall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dancehall), [rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rap), [ragga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ragga), [R\&B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_R\&B) and [dance-pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dance-pop).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMbabazi2008-377]



Dance is an integral aspect of the Rwandan culture

Traditional arts and crafts are produced throughout the country, although most originated as functional items rather than purely for decoration. Woven baskets and bowls are especially common, notably the basket style of the [agaseke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agaseke).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200768–70-378] *[Imigongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imigongo)*, a unique cow dung art, is produced in the southeast of Rwanda, with a history dating back to when the region was part of the independent [Gisaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisaka?action=edit\&redlink=1) kingdom. The dung is mixed with natural soils of various colours and painted into patterned ridges to form geometric shapes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth2006243–244-379] Other crafts include pottery and wood carving.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200631-380] Traditional housing styles make use of locally available materials; circular or rectangular mud homes with grass-thatched roofs (known as *nyakatsi*) are the most common. The government has initiated a programme to replace these with more modern materials such as corrugated iron.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENtambara2009-381][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200775-382]

Photograph depicting baskets hung up for sale

Collection of different types of woven baskets

Rwanda does not have a long history of written literature, but there is a strong oral tradition ranging from poetry to [folk stories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_stories). Many of the country's moral values and details of history have been passed down through the generations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing2007105-383] The most famous Rwandan literary figure was [Alexis Kagame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexis_Kagame) (1912–1981), who carried out and published research into oral traditions as well as writing his own poetry.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200629-384] The Rwandan Genocide resulted in the emergence of a literature of witness accounts, essays and fiction by a new generation of writers such as [Benjamin Sehene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Sehene) and [Mfuranzima Fred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Mfuranzima). A number of films have been produced about the Rwandan Genocide, including the [Golden Globe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Globe)-nominated *[Hotel Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hotel_Rwanda)*, *[100 Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/100_Days_\(2001_film\))*, *[Shake Hands with the Devil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shake_Hands_with_the_Devil_\(2007_film\))*, *[Sometimes in April](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sometimes_in_April)*, and *[Shooting Dogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_Dogs)*, the last four having been filmed in Rwanda and having featured survivors as cast members.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMilmo2006-385][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFegley201655–56-386]

Fourteen regular [national holidays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Rwanda) are observed throughout the year,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGovernment_of_Rwanda_(I)-387] with others occasionally inserted by the government. The week following Genocide Memorial Day on 7 April is designated an official week of mourning.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWatsonRenziViggiani201025-388] The victory for the RPF over the Hutu extremists is celebrated as [Liberation Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Day) on 4 July. The last Saturday of each month is *[umuganda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umuganda)*, a national morning of mandatory [community service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_service) lasting from 8 am to 11 am, during which all able bodied people between 18 and 65 are expected to carry out community tasks such as cleaning streets or building homes for vulnerable people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERwanda_Governance_Board-389] Most normal services close down during *umuganda*, and public transportation is limited.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERwanda_Governance_Board-389]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Cuisine of Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Rwanda)



Brochettes, meat on a stick

Rwanda's cuisine consists of [staple foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staple_food) largely produced by [subsistence agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsistence_agriculture) (e.g. [plantains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibitoke), [pulses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legume), [sweet potatoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweet_potato), [cassava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassava)) and reflects other [East African](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Community) foods (e.g. [mandazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandazi), [chapati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chapati)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200781-390] For those who live near lakes and have access to fish, [tilapia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tilapia) is popular.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200781-390] The [potato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potato), thought to have been introduced to Rwanda by German and Belgian [colonialists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_European_colonization_wave_\(19th_century%E2%80%9320th_century\)), is very popular.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713-391] [Ugali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ugali), locally known as *Ubugari* (or *umutsima*) is common, a paste made from cassava or [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize) and water to form a [porridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porridge)-like consistency that is eaten throughout East Africa and across sub-Saharan Africa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAuzias200774-392] *[Isombe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isombe)* is made from mashed cassava leaves and can be served with dried fish, [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice), ugali, potatoes etc.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713-391] Lunch is usually a [buffet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffet) known as *mélange*, consisting of the above staples and sometimes meat.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200654–55-393]



Shortcake Mandazi (originated in neighbouring [Tanzania](30118.md))

[Brochettes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brochette) are the most popular food when eating out in the evening, usually made from goat but sometimes [tripe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tripe), beef, or fish.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBriggsBooth200654–55-393] In rural areas, many bars have a brochette seller responsible for tending and slaughtering the goats, skewering and [barbecuing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbecue) the meat, and serving it with grilled bananas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnyango2010-394]

However, many children in Rwanda are [malnourished](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malnourished) (1/3 for children under five).[^cite_note-395]

Milk, particularly in a fermented [yoghurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yogurt) form called *[ikivuguto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikivuguto?action=edit\&redlink=1)*, is a common drink throughout the country.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENzabuheraheza2005-396] Other drinks include a traditional beer called *[ikigage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ikigage?action=edit\&redlink=1)*, made from [sorghum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorghum), and *[urwagwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urwagwa)*, made from bananas, and a soft drink called *[umutobe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umutobe?action=edit\&redlink=1)* which consists of banana juice; these popular drinks feature in traditional rituals and ceremonies.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdekunle200713-391]

The major drinks manufacturer in Rwanda is [Bralirwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bralirwa), which was established in the 1950s, a [Heineken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heineken) partner, and is now listed on the [Rwandan Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwandan_Stock_Exchange).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(I)-397] Bralirwa manufactures [soft drink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_drink) products from [The Coca-Cola Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Coca-Cola_Company), under licence, including [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola), [Fanta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fanta), and [Sprite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sprite_\(soft_drink\)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(II)-398] and a range of beers including [Primus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primus_beer), [Mützig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%BCtzig_beer), [Amstel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amstel_Brewery), and [Turbo King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turbo_King?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBralirwa_(III)-399] In 2009 a new brewery, [Brasseries des Mille Collines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brasseries_des_Mille_Collines?action=edit\&redlink=1) (BMC) opened, manufacturing [Skol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skol) beer and a local version known as [Skol Gatanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skol_Gatanu?action=edit\&redlink=1);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENgarambe2012-400] BMC is now owned by Belgian company [Unibra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unibra).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECraig2012-401] [East African Breweries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_African_Breweries) also operate in the country, importing [Guinness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness), [Tusker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tusker_\(African_beer\)), and [Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_\(beer\)?action=edit\&redlink=1), as well as [whisky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whisky) and [spirits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distilled_beverage).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKezio-Musoke2014-402]

### Sport

Main article: [Sport in Rwanda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Rwanda)



[Adrien Niyonshuti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrien_Niyonshuti), "one of the most famous people in Rwanda",

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECyclingNews.com2012-403]

&#x20;competing in the [cross-country mountain biking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross-country_mountain_biking) event at the 2012 Summer Olympics

The Rwandan government, through its Sports Development Policy, promotes sport as a strong avenue for "development and peace building",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMINISPOC201218-404] and the government has made commitments to advancing the use of sport for a variety of development objectives, including education.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcCrackenColucci201486–90-405] The most popular sports in Rwanda are [association football](10568.md), [volleyball](32558.md), [basketball](3921.md), [athletics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletics_\(sport\)) and [Paralympic sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paralympic_sports).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENdengeye2014125–128-406] [Cricket](25675557.md) has been growing in popularity,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAglietti2014-407] as a result of refugees returned from Kenya, where they had learned to play the game.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_News''2014-408]

[Cycling](5931.md), traditionally seen largely as a mode of transport in Rwanda, is also growing in popularity [as a sport](857311.md);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoyeSmithNicholsonStewart2015206-409] and Team Rwanda have been the subject of a book, *Land of Second Chances: The Impossible Rise of Rwanda's Cycling Team* and a film, *[Rising from Ashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rising_from_Ashes_\(film\))*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERobbins2013-410][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWillgoss2014-411] The [UCI Road World Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_UCI_Road_World_Championships) took place in Kigali in September 2025.



The [Gahanga Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_Cricket_Stadium)

Rwandans have been competing at the [Olympic Games](22576.md) since 1984,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_Sport''2012-412] and the [Paralympic Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paralympic_Games) since 2004.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_Paralympic_Committee2015-413] The country sent seven competitors to the [2012 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Summer_Olympics) in London, representing it in athletics, [swimming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swimming_\(sport\)), [mountain biking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_biking) and [judo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judo),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_Sport''2012-412] and 15 competitors to the [London Summer Paralympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Summer_Paralympics) to compete in athletics, [powerlifting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paralympic_powerlifting) and [sitting volleyball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sitting_volleyball).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_Paralympic_Committee2015-413] The country has also participated in the [Commonwealth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Games) since joining the [Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations) in 2009.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE''BBC_Sport''2010-414][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOffice_of_the_Prime_Minister2014-415] The country's [national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_national_basketball_team) has been growing in prominence since the mid-2000s, with the men's team qualifying for the final stages of the [African Basketball Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Basketball_Championship) four times in a row since 2007.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBishumba2015-416] The country bid unsuccessfully to host the [2013 tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_African_Basketball_Championship).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMackay2009-417][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_Basketball_Federation2011-418] Rwanda's [national football team](1145307.md) has appeared in the [African Cup of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Cup_of_Nations) once, in the [2004 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_African_Cup_of_Nations) of the tournament,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarlin2003-419] but narrowly failed to advance beyond the group stages.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECopnall2004-420] The team have failed to qualify for the competition since, and have never qualified for the [World Cup](11370.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontague201467-421] Rwanda's highest domestic football competition is the [Rwanda National Football League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_National_Football_League);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMugabeKamasa2014-422] as of 2015, the dominant team is [APR FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APR_FC) of Kigali, having won 13 of the last 17 championships.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchöggl2015-423] Rwandan clubs participate in the [Kagame Interclub Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kagame_Interclub_Cup) for Central and East African teams, sponsored since 2002 by President Kagame.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECECAFA-424]
